What was **not** observed in-window: a translated page landing back in the shared reader. The tracked `ReaderHostTranslationActionsTrial` passes `1/1` on 237 (it only asserts the shared menu exposes and dispatches the host translation actions), and its own captures sharpen the picture: the `dispatched` frame shows the busy status 「正在转录并翻译当前页…」 (the shared surface is waiting on the host action) but the later `followup` frame still reads 「翻译当前页」 — not the applied 「显示原图」 — with `rkit-image-viewport`/`rkit-part-0-whole`/`rkit-original-image` unchanged and no failure panel, so within that window the Torii call never returned a result. Subsequent 237 probes are inconclusive on the route outcome rather than negative: a later long-window retry could not re-observe the dispatch because EH content failed to load in that window (`0 / 0`), and two content-loaded probes that tried to drive the action by raw menu coordinates did not re-trigger the `torii_whole_page_request` log at all — so the reliable dispatch path is the tracked trial's component lookup, and the round-trip result should be observed by extending a run driven through that component, not raw coordinates. Separately, the on-device Torii credential was verified live host-side: `GET https://api.toriitranslate.com/api/credits` with the device key returns **HTTP 200** (`{"credits":5390.78}`), so the route is not auth-failed; it is simply slow/long-running. Hence the item advances from "not exercised through the shared surface" to "the shared surface does reach the real non-local provider (a real `torii_whole_page_request` was sent), and the key is live; the round-trip result (a rendered translated page back in the shared reader) is still unproven and needs a component-driven run that waits long enough". No setting was changed (read-only: the persisted Torii route was used as-is); no product source changed. Artifacts: `.hvigor/outputs/nexte-237-translation-2594d6f/run/{actions,dispatched,followup,retried}.{json,png}` (hilog `torii_whole_page_request`), `.hvigor/outputs/nexte-237-torii-e2e/run/{menu,mid,late}.{json,png}`, `.hvigor/outputs/nexte-237-torii-long/run/{s60,s150,s270}.{json,png}`, `.hvigor/outputs/nexte-237-torii-e2e2/run/{a,s120,s240}.{json,png}`, `.hvigor/outputs/nexte-237-torii-menu/run/menu.json`.

However the host chain then rejected the returned image: `[NextNSharedTranslation] unavailable ... error=Error: Torii whole-page image MIME type does not match its bytes`, and the shared surface truthfully settled to its host copy `翻译失败` (`applied=false hostOutcome=翻译失败`). Reproduced identically across four runs. Classification: this is **not** a shared-reader regression — the check lives in the shared translation service (`ComicTranslationRuntimeService.runToriiWholePage` -> `ToriiWholePageRenderBackend.decodeImageDataUrl`) whose decode/MIME-validation region is **identical** between NextN and NextE (the files differ only in a diagnostic-logger import and the multipart boundary prefix, not in the check that rejects the payload; the same route succeeds on NextE), and NextN's **legacy** reader calls the **same** `ComicTranslationRuntimeService.runReaderPage` (`ReaderPage.ets:4661`), so the legacy reader would hit the same gate. The distinguishing factor is the configured Torii model on 237 (`the device's configured Torii model` for NextN vs `the configured Torii model` for NextE), i.e. a provider-payload outcome on the user-configured cloud route, not new shared code. CORRECTION (2026-09-18, later same-day probe): the model is NOT the differentiator - 237's existing NextN Torii render-cache entries (`a host project id` p1, `a host project id` p0/p1; read-only `(purged cache probe)`) were also produced with `the device's configured Torii model`, i.e. the same model that failed here has rendered Torii pages successfully before, so this is an intermittent/page-dependent provider-payload outcome rather than a model-specific defect. The shared surface reaches the provider and truthfully surfaces the host chain's outcome; the host-side payload rejection is a pre-existing host/cloud condition, reproduced 4/4 on gallery 678049 page 1, and re-confirmed on gallery 678049 page 2 (a different uncached page, same host failure and same model), so it is gallery/content-level rather than a single-page artifact. Artifacts: `.hvigor/outputs/nextn-237-rt-compact2/` (p1), `.hvigor/outputs/nextn-237-rt-page2/` (p2). The probe therefore asserts only the shared-reader contract (dispatch to the real provider) and records the host outcome honestly. Artifacts: `.hvigor/outputs/nextn-237-rt-compact2/run/run-metadata.json` (clean 28 s pass with fresh installs; hilog request/response + host failure), `(purged read-only route probe)` (persisted route). No setting or user data changed.   - **NextE runtime-continuity write trial is brittle when the durable page already equals its target (2026-09-17, 197)**: on the pinned `f450aad` candidate the write phase failed with `NextE Reader did not turn from page 3` at `ReaderRuntimeContinuity.test.ets:287`. The recovered failure capture `.hvigor/outputs/nexte-f450aad-continuity/03-caps/write-stuck-3.png` shows the shared reader correctly in **single-page mode at `4 / 46`** with the real page art — i.e. it is **not** a product defect and **not** the mode drift I initially suspected. Root cause is the trial’s own precondition: it reads `start = sharedVisiblePage()` and loops `while current !== target` with `target = 3`; when the device’s durable page is already index 3, `start === target`, the loop body never runs, and the guard `if (current === start) throw "did not turn from page N"` fires even though nothing was wrong to turn. The earlier record passed only because the durable page was 0 then. This is now RESOLVED by the shipped fix — `3018bcb2` replaced the fixed-target loop with the direction-agnostic `turnOnce` helper that taps a real non-menu zone and accepts whichever page it lands on, and that commit's pinned `f450aad` run passed (`resumed=45 expected=45 coldStart=legacy reset=legacy`); the stale fixed-target note here is superseded and kept only as history.

- Legacy-reader **super-resolution page-turn crash reproduced, root-caused and fixed (2026-09-18, NextE, 197 ALN-AL80 1260x2720)** - the user reported that with super resolution on, the Reader crashed on the first page turn. A real on-device crash stack was captured; the defect is confirmed by that stack, not by theory. Attribution of the packaging loss (when/who) is NOT established - see the scope bullet.
  - **Reproduction + capture method:** a new `ReaderLegacyEnhancementCrashTrial` (`entry/src/ohosTest`) drives the real route - enable super resolution, History -> Detail -> the Detail Read action -> the legacy `reader_key_surface` - then swipes six pages. One checked manifest installs `-r` main+ohosTest, force-stops, runs the class through `aa test`, then reads the crash out of hilog and the persisted `/data/log/hilog` archives. The earlier failure to capture was a method error (the volatile hilog ring held only ~8k lines and the stale crash had already scrolled out); the fix is to reproduce inside the same run and read immediately.
  - **Pre-fix evidence:** the run ended `TestFinished-ResultMsg: App died`. hilog shows `load libreader_enhancement.so failed ... No such file or directory`, `export objects of native so is undefined, so name is libreader_enhancement.so`, then `@Monitor exception caught for onActiveIndexChanged  TypeError: Cannot read property setRequestActive of undefined`, `AppKit: com.erosteam.nexte is about to exit due to RuntimeError / Error type:TypeError`, and the AMS kill `reason=Js Error`. Artifacts: `.hvigor/outputs/nexte-legacy-sr-crash-197/run/run-metadata.json`.
  - **Two independent defects:** (1) the local signed build profile had dropped the `reader_enhancement` source-module registration, so the produced HAP packed no `libs/arm64-v8a/libreader_enhancement.so` (the built HAP listed only `libc++_shared`, `libimageknifepro`, `libomp`, `libyuv`); the committed `HEAD:build-profile.json5` still has the registration, so the loss was confined to the machine-local override. (2) `shared/src/main/ets/services/ReaderNativeSuperResolution.ets` `setRequestActive` called the native module without a guard, unlike the neighbouring `setInteractionPaused`, so an absent/failed native module threw `TypeError` from inside a `@Monitor` page-change observer and the framework killed the process.
  - **Fix:** re-register `reader_enhancement` in the local build profile, and wrap `setRequestActive` so a missing/failed native module degrades instead of throwing from a Reader page-change observer. The committed source change is the guard (`shared/.../ReaderNativeSuperResolution.ets`) plus the new trial; the profile registration was already present on `HEAD`.
  - **Post-fix evidence (narrow):** after the fix the signed HAP packs `libs/arm64-v8a/libreader_enhancement.so` (8,763,536 bytes, verified by unzip), and the same trial reports `Tests run: 1, Failure: 0, Error: 0, Pass: 1`, `[ReaderLegacyEnhancementCrashTrial] survived 6 turns`, `ReaderEnhancement: foreground interaction pause=1 / pause=0`, and `ReaderEnhancement: native ncnn runtime loaded`. That asserts only that the Reader surface is still alive after six swipes and that the native runtime loaded - it does **not** verify six distinct page turns, page numbers, or an applied enhanced image. No `TypeError`, no `load libreader_enhancement.so failed`, no process kill in the run. **The run-metadata head reports status: failed** because the protocol postflight hit a UnicodeDecodeError while reading a log line; the `aa test` trial itself reported Pass 1 and the app stayed alive, but the whole protocol is not a clean pass. Artifacts: `.hvigor/outputs/nexte-legacy-sr-crash-197-fixed/run/run-metadata.json`. NextE commit `c3af52f6`.

  **RESIDUAL CLOSED (2026-09-19, 103, NextE `757a7fa3` reader-kit `2594d6f`, `install -r`)**: the earlier post-fix evidence only asserted that the Reader surface was **alive** after six swipes, not that pages actually turned. The trial now reads the legacy reader's own per-page tag (`reader-thumb-reader-surface-<scope>-route-<epoch>-page-<index>`, the id the accept path already publishes) and asserts at least one visible page change across the six turns, and it swipes along the **persisted** reading-mode axis/direction instead of assuming a horizontal LTR reader. On 103 (real Home gallery, SR enabled) it passes **`Tests run: 1, Failure: 0, Error: 0, Pass: 1`** with `page=14` after turn 6 and `pageChanges=6` — i.e. six real page turns on real content with super resolution on, not mere survival. (The first run of the strengthened trial reported `page=2` for all six turns because the persisted 103 reading mode turns pages along the **vertical** axis, so a horizontal swipe could not turn anything; that is a probe-axis bug, now fixed by reading `connectReadMode().mode` and swiping that axis, and the captures `legacy-turn-1|3|5` confirmed the same page `1 / 37` in that run. Which of TOP_TO_BOTTOM/VERTICAL the persisted mode is was not separately captured.) Artifacts: `.hvigor/outputs/nexte-103-srturn-2594d6f-b/run/run-metadata.json`; the discarded horizontal-axis run and its frames are kept at `.hvigor/outputs/nexte-103-srturn-2594d6f/run/` and `.hvigor/outputs/nexte-103-srturn-recv/run/`.
  - **Scope and boundary (narrow):** the crash is a real legacy-Reader enhancement-path defect, fixed at the shared native boundary both hosts use. The following are NOT established by the current evidence and must not be asserted: (a) there is **no historical evidence for a pre-existing-product-defect / not-a-shared-reader-regression label** - when and by whom the local signed-profile override lost the `reader_enhancement` registration is **not determined**, so a development/test-introduced loss that also affects the ordinary entry **cannot be ruled out**; (b) NextN having the `.so` packed and guarded call sites in source shows the risk profile differs there, but it is **not a device proof that NextN never crashes**; (c) the resemblance to the NextN v1.0.5 packaging regression (`setInteractionPaused of undefined`) is a resemblance, not a shared provenance claim. **2026-09-19 scope decision (not a new counterexample, so no symmetry run):** the NextE callee guard was re-checked against NextN source rather than re-opened for symmetry. The fix is host-local — it edits NextE's own `shared/src/main/ets/services/ReaderNativeSuperResolution.ets`, which is not the `third_party/reader-kit` the three hosts share, so it cannot reach NextN at all. NextN's own equivalent surface is already defended at both call sites: `ReaderSuperResolutionService.releaseOwner` wraps `ReaderNativeSuperResolution.setRequestActive` in try/catch (lines 206-210) and `applyInteractionPause` wraps `setInteractionPaused` (lines 256-259), and NextN's built HAP packs `libs/arm64-v8a/libreader_enhancement.so` (8,763,536 bytes) — the two conditions whose absence crashed NextE. So no NextN legacy-SR behavior is left without evidence by this defect; the NextN legacy path is covered by its existing ordinary-entry/legacy-fallback records, and the crash class is reproduced-and-fixed on the host that actually had it. If a future non-test edit removes either guard or the module registration, that reopens this row. The fix is bounded and evidence-backed; it does not reopen the shared-reader parity matrix, and no matrix re-run is warranted for this wording.

  - **Commit map:** NextE `c3af52f6` (legacy super-resolution page-turn crash guard) and `ed941768` (built-in custom-profile name auto-recovery) were cherry-picked from `codex/shared-reader-refactor` onto a temporary local integration branch `codex/integrate-legacy-sr-and-cpn-fixes` built from `main` (`d6730058`), producing `8f918e43` and `ed91738e`. Local `main` was fast-forwarded `d6730058` to `f3b824c6` (not pushed).

Re-added it, then built the integrated `main` candidate: the signed HAP packs `libs/arm64-v8a/libreader_enhancement.so` (8,763,536 bytes) and carries the `custom_profiles_name_repair` path. This is a build-entry fix, not a proof that any previously shipped artifact was correct.
  - **Main-checkout signed profile closed too (2026-09-18):** the gap the earlier bullet described was only fixed inside the throwaway integration worktree; the user s primary NextE checkout (`/Users/honjow/git/NextE`) still had a signed `build-profile.json5` override that omitted `reader_enhancement`. Git fast-forwarding `main` does not touch a skip-worktree override, so a build from the main checkout would again ship without the native module. Added the same single module registration to the main checkout s `build-profile.json5` and its `build-profile.local.json5` (signing and every other setting preserved; no secret printed), then ran the project signed-build entrypoint `scripts/build_hvigor_signed.sh` from the main checkout: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, and the signed HAP from the main checkout now packs `libs/arm64-v8a/libreader_enhancement.so` (8,763,536 bytes). Artifact: `/Users/honjow/git/NextE/entry/build/default/outputs/default/entry-default-signed.hap`. So both the temporary integration package and the primary checkout build entry now carry the native library; this remains a build-entry fix, not evidence about any previously shipped artifact.

Artifacts: `.hvigor/outputs/nexte-main-integration-197/`, `.hvigor/outputs/nexte-main-sr-197/`. The earlier failing run (missing `nexte-root-tab-4`) is retained at `.hvigor/outputs/nexte-main-integration-197/`.

NextN `codex/shared-reader-refactor` merged `main` at `f757f0e3` (parents `5fd33b5f` + `a85f3ca9`); the three translation-prompt updates are present and a clean rebuild passes with `libreader_enhancement.so` packed. Both merges resolved the `List.test.ets` registration so both sides keep their trials. Koma needed no sync and was not touched.

- **NextN built-in Home SubTab names can no longer be persisted as a resource key (2026-09-19)** - the user asked for the same protection NextE already has. `AppStrings.get()` returns the key itself when a string resource is not yet resolvable, and the seed / `ensureBuiltins` paths stored that resolved-or-key value in the durable `home_subtabs.name`, so an unresolved launch could persist `home_source_latest` and sync it. This was NOT currently polluted on the devices checked (see the device note), and no production data was injected.
  - **Fix (NextN contract preserved):** a new `shared/src/main/ets/settings/HomeSubtabNames.ets` holds the reserved uuid to resource-key map and refuses an unresolved key as a name (`isKeyName`, `defaultNameOrEmpty`, `assertPersistable`). `seedDefaults`/`ensureBuiltins` keep the original normalization when the resource resolves (so an in-app language switch still re-localizes built-ins) and only skip the assignment when it does not, so a valid stored label is never overwritten by a key and never blanked. A reserved row whose label is still missing is not persisted (restore returns before writing) so a later restore with resources ready seeds the real label. `HomeSubtabRepository` refuses to write a raw reserved key and repairs only an exact reserved uuid whose stored name still equals its own key, advancing `last_edit_time` past any incoming future clock under `WHERE name = ? AND last_edit_time = ?`; the repair runs on restore, on sync apply (`importRecords`, same transaction) and on sync refresh. An unresolved inbound remote key row is skipped (never stored) so one stale polluted row cannot block the rest of the sync, and the existing tombstone/clock conflict decision is unchanged. Custom labels, order, hidden, query and tombstones are untouched.
  - **Real-call verification (not a mirror):** `scripts/verify_home_subtab_names.cjs` loads the actual post-fix ArkTS modules (types stripped) against an isolated in-memory SQLite and drives `HomeSubtabSettings.restore`, `HomeSubtabRepository.importRecords/replaceAll/loadProfiles` directly. 12 cases PASS: first seed with unresolved resources persists nothing and a ready retry seeds real labels; a pre-existing custom empty name does not block restore; a reserved key row is repaired while unrelated rows stay byte-identical and repair is idempotent; the repository refuses a raw reserved key; an obsolete polluted remote cannot override a newer custom name; an accepted polluted remote is normalized on import while keeping the conflict clock; a remote tombstone is not resurrected; an unresolved inbound row is skipped without blocking others and reconciled on a later ready pass. `scripts/test_home_subtab_contract.mjs` also passes (contract-level assertions).
  - **Device note (197, read-only):** the current real `home_subtabs` rows are `builtin-latest=最新, builtin-popular=热门, starter-language-chinese=中文, starter-highly-favorited=收藏 K`, `key_names=0` - the default names are currently correct on this device, so this closes a latent hazard rather than an observed corruption. `NhHomeSubtabNameVerifyTrial` records it.
  - **Commit map:** implemented in a fresh local integration worktree `codex/integrate-home-subtab-name-fix` from NextN `main` (`a85f3ca9`) as `d2c3e9d6` (5 files: the two settings/storage files, the new `HomeSubtabNames`, the contract script and the real-call verifier); local `main` fast-forwarded `a85f3ca9 -> d2c3e9d6` (not pushed); merged back into the NextN reader branch `codex/shared-reader-refactor` at `07b98d03` (merge, no rebase, no push). The merge carries no `reader-kit` migration and no unrelated WIP; the reader branch P1 trial (`ReaderContinuousLegacyParity`) and the untracked `scripts/hdc-guard.sh` were preserved via stash/pop. The signed HAP builds and packs `libreader_enhancement.so`; `HomeSubtabNames`/`home_subtabs_names_unresolved`/`home_subtab_remote_key_unresolved` are present in the integrated build.
  - **Not verified here:** the current contract already normalizes a built-in label to the current resource value on every resolve, so an existing valid stored label may still be re-localized on a language switch (intended). No cloud round-trip or second device was exercised for this slice; the local-call and 197 read-only evidence is the scope of this record.

- **P1 continuous same-state parity verified (NextN, 2026-09-19, 197 ALN-AL80)** - the board still called continuous same-state parity unproven. A new `ReaderContinuousLegacyParityTrial` (`entry/src/ohosTest`) drives the real ordinary entry: set mode to VERTICAL, then for each backend select it in-process and open the same gallery 678049 through Home (我的 -> 历史记录 -> Detail -> Read). Both sides render continuous at the same page: Shared `rkit-reading-surface` + `rkit-continuous-list` + `rkit-continuous-page-0` (`1 / 14`) and Legacy `legacy-reader-surface` (`1 / 14`). The visible page image has an identical x extent and size (`123..436`, 313x569) on both; y differs by 51 px, exactly the Shared top chrome (`rkit-chrome-top`) inset - the documented Shared(chrome shown) vs Legacy(immersive) presentation trade-off, not a geometry/experience difference. So at the same state the two backends show the same page at the same place and extent. Artifacts: `.hvigor/outputs/nextn-p1-continuous-197b/run/{shared.json,legacy.json}`. Scope: single NextN page-identity/geometry at one gallery on the phone; it does not by itself re-accept every continuous interaction, which stay covered by their own records.
  - Trial-only change (`entry/src/ohosTest/ets/test/ReaderContinuousLegacyParity.test.ets`): the Shared wait keys off `rkit-continuous-list` rather than a hard-coded page index, and the trial runs on the reader branch. No product source changed for P1.

- **P13 first bounded device run (2026-09-19, 197, NextN, isolated endpoint)**: the new `ReaderSelfHostedPublishTrial` opened the shared Reader on real content and the More menu, and the menu contained only `rkit-reload-source` / `rkit-image-info` / `rkit-reset-zoom` (no `rkit-host-action-translate-page`) across 20 menu reopen attempts. Root cause is the lab s `translationConfiguration().enabled` being false: the shared self-hosted chain still needs a **configured manga LLM text-translator source** on the device (the same prerequisite the NextE host translation round-trip uses). This is an environment prerequisite, not a shared-surface or seam gap - the isolated endpoint override itself is wired and compiled. Artifacts: `.hvigor/outputs/nextn-selfhosted-publish-197/`, `.hvigor/outputs/nextn-selfhosted-publish-197b/`. Stop condition met: one bounded run produced the classification; no platform was built and no user setting was written.
